## First actual public fixture provenance acceptance (2026-10-24)

[PR #37](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/37) merged at `cd71f953dff55f776826aeefd15541ed0bce57b4`. [Run 40071224039](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40071224039), tested SHA `5925bfb3b1d484c31f3d45018afc07fbfe823871`, successfully completed both **real external** source jobs and the separate offline collector tests ([run 40071224040](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40071224040)). The Commons job downloaded actual licensed `File:Example.ogg` bytes (checked against API size and SHA256), generated a v1 .torrent and magnet, and verified exact infohash agreement. Archive validated a real `BigBuckBunny_124` Archive BitTorrent derivative plus license, infohash and magnet, without downloading its ~421 MiB media. Short-lived actions artifacts `chunks-commons-real-source` and `chunks-archive-real-source` were successfully uploaded. The previous live failure was traced to UTM query arguments on an official upload.wikimedia.org asset URL; specifically allowed tracking keys are now stripped and unknown query arguments rejected. **Passed source acquisition and identity only**. No live rTorrent Add, actual peer download or Android visual/device proof.

## Android SAF .torrent picker proposal

PR #40 proposes actual on-device `.torrent` file inspection through standard Android SAF file picker. Synthetic parser tests must pass before merge. A successful parser build validates source-format preview only, not binary metainfo RPC upload, real external file read on a handset or full Add success. The existing Add draft remains non-submitting.

## Actual Kotlin adapter protected Seedhost smoke candidate

PR #42 provides a manual-only, default-branch protected read-only Kotlin smoke with exact Seedhost URL/username/password secret contract. A normal green public PR is a compile check and **not** evidence of live adapter compatibility. Record a real manual `workflow_dispatch` run and tested commit SHA before claiming this gate. It does not test Android device TLS or any Add/move/remove behavior.

## Two-stage Add UI candidate

PR #44 integrates earlier readback APIs with an in-app Add workflow. It requires CI and device testing. A successfully assembled APK proves compilation, **not** a complete verified device Add or server authentication. Outcomes are displayed without conflating acknowledgement, pending, duplicate and uncertain states; source is chosen explicitly if both magnet and .torrent are present.

## Controlled real-engine Add/lifecycle integration candidate

PR #46 proposes real rTorrent 0.9.8 stopped binary Add, duplicate protection and start/stop via the exact Kotlin adapter and an isolated nginx SCGI gateway. Tests are scoped to `127.0.0.1:18080`, use a throwaway path and no credentials. Once green, record exact tested SHA and job result. This is not modern build or authenticated Seedhost proof, and does not prove physical bytes or relocation.

## Add destination-parent compatibility caveat

The rTorrent documentation differentiates `d.directory.set` from `d.directory_base.set` for multi-file torrents; the latter can flatten/remove the transfer-name subfolder. PR #47 corrects new-Add semantics to parent-directory selection and multi-file-aware readback, with offline negative tests. Prior real-engine runs that cover only single-file torrents do not establish multi-file destination integrity, and this remains a distinct acceptance gate.

## Transfer lifecycle controls UI candidate

PR #49 wires existing JVM-tested hash-targeted lifecycle operations into native Compose Cards, requiring explicit confirmation and server read-back. Build pending verification; no physical Android journey or production Seedhost lifecycle write has been observed. Stale snapshot blocks control, and ambiguous result is never reported as verified. Distinct from Add/Remove/File move acceptance gates.

## Exact-hash session removal API candidate

PR #50 adds an allowlisted `d.erase` call preflighted against the main-view hash, expected torrent name and directory. `AlreadyAbsent`, `VerifiedRemoved`, `PendingRemoval` and `Uncertain` are separate. The synthetic fixture tests and Android build require exact CI evidence; no live Seedhost or disposable real rTorrent removal has been observed by this PR. No file deletion claimed, and rTorrent event hooks may alter auxiliary tied metainfo: https://rtorrent-docs.readthedocs.io/en/latest/cmd-ref.html#d-erase. Keep binary payload deletion under a separate companion/filesystem verification plan.

## October 25 read-only import and identity acceptance

The [#110](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/110) import bridge uses Android provider documents, caps copied bytes before calling read-only ZIP member-type and inflated CRC validators, stores staging only in private cache, and attempts to delete that temporary copy in a `finally` block. Instrumentation tests assert staging copies do not remain after ordinary, malicious and over-budget specimens. **This does not establish resilience to process death or failed filesystem deletion.** ZipFile directory preflight plus CRC verification remain safe *observations*, not permission to write any output file. Android visual baseline captures only the server connection screen. Never call this extraction or imply remote torrents can be opened through a phone picker.

The [#111](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/111) rTorrent list check aligns snapshot identity validation with hash-addressed commands, prior to existing case-insensitive duplicate-identity rejection. Fixture success is not evidence that the designated remote server reports compliant IDs, v2 Add is supported or destructive commands work on Seedhost.

## October 25 stopped-Add metainfo count guard

[#112](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/112) merged `e7ed866d2401fb1f5e5178f6d405d28e8b3b72c3`. Exact tested source `d2122fc523ff7934455dfec4eced3d151f7327a0` passed [Android/JVM 40229540520](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40229540520). The pure v1 bencode preview computes `ceil(total declared logical bytes / piece length)` without overflowing and checks the number of supplied SHA-1 piece digests before any network Add. Fixtures cover valid final partial pieces, malformed counts, empty/huge totals and inconsistent multi-file metainfo. This is a JVM metainfo validity gate, **not** a guarantee that server import succeeds or that remote data/pieces match; no BitTorrent v2/hybrid, real Seedhost, 12k-item queue or physical Android proof follows from it.

A later ZIP read-only review prunes only old app-cache entries with the review's generated filename pattern, after a 24-hour grace. The Android instrumentation proves old normal temporary files are reclaimed, while fresh/foreign/future-timestamp entries and matching directory are preserved. No result here proves a killed-process replay, symlink-race resistance, actual user picker screenshot, storage allocation quota, durable output journal, torrent payload deletion or extraction.

## October 25 manual ZIP extraction executor (model)

[#119](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/119) merged `41184414d0585596536b59e6a0fbbb8793fb1b7f`. Exact source `ecc580073b6070d1c6eda73c09be93ced12c387f` passed [Android/JVM 40236549474](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40236549474); before push the four `Zip*Test` classes (41 tests) also passed in a plain Kotlin/JVM 21 harness. `ZipExtractor` verifies first, checks headroom, journals write-ahead, re-checks actual bytes/ratio/CRC while writing into a private partial folder and publishes by one non-replacing rename to a **new** folder. JVM fault injection covers provider name refusal, ENOSPC-style write failure, cancellation, simulated process death mid-write (reconcile rollback, idempotent) and after rename (recognised as published), and refusal to remove unjournaled files. Platform-neutral `File` target only: not Android SAF, UI, device or vendor-provider evidence.

## October 25 local engine core (libtorrent4j)

[#122](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/122) merged `f6996fb002e341b39c488abe75d0f9f2106e4b8d`; exact source `e467493f99ac33d59151e7306fa1c08a44509ec2` passed [Android/JVM 40241820196](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40241820196) and [API35 40241820180](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40241820180).

`engine/LocalTorrentEngine` wraps libtorrent4j `2.1.0-39`. JVM tests (desktop Linux binary) run two real sessions on loopback with DHT/LSD/UPnP/NAT-PMP disabled: a generated 1.3 MB multi-file torrent is downloaded byte-for-byte into an explicitly chosen folder and the seeder's upload counter reaches the payload size; stopped Add writes nothing; pause/resume report `VERIFIED` only after readback (the first local attempt exposed that libtorrent applies them asynchronously, so immediate readback was wrong); duplicate, invalid metadata, missing/relative folders and malformed identities are refused; session-only removal keeps bytes. Five consecutive local runs passed after waiting for the asynchronous stats tick. An API35 instrumentation test repeats the loopback transfer using the Android x86_64 native library (passed on CI). The first CI heads crashed the *JVM* unit-test process with exit 139 after the engine suite had passed 5/5: libtorrent4j installs native signal handlers, and later ordinary tests (deep XML parsing) hit the JVM's own SIGSEGV-based stack guards/implicit null checks without chaining. Reproduced locally (3/3), fixed by preloading the JDK's `libjsig.so` for Gradle test JVMs (67/67 engine+rpc tests then pass); an initial classpath-order theory was wrong. Android ART chains signals through its own sigchain. The Android CI job now prints any JVM fatal-error report and the completed suites when JVM tests crash. Not evidence for: UI, foreground service/background lifecycle, resume data, SAF storage, real Internet peers, arm64 physical device.

"On this phone (experimental)" adds v1 `.torrent` review → stopped Add into Chunks' app folder → Start/Stop with verified/uncertain readback → confirmed session-only removal; the engine starts only on the first explicit Add. Component tests cover review content and remove confirmation; screenshots are CI-validated PNGs, not inspected here. No device Internet download has been observed.

[#124](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/124) merged `8aaef6cfc3a3b38d145483e6bda389554e9bc549` (exact source `dac8a5ce51fac905610ff80e397a2e59093fbf5f`, [Android/JVM 40242949674](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40242949674), [API35 40242949670](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40242949670)): optional `stateDirectory` persists one atomically replaced resume file per transfer (written at Add, refreshed via `save_resume_data` alerts, every ~30 s on screen, and at close); restore re-adds every well-formed file **stopped and unmanaged**, verifies identity against the file name, and reports unusable files without modifying them. JVM: a completed loopback download survives engine restart at 100% without re-download and comes back stopped; wrong-identity and garbage resume files are reported and preserved; removal deletes only that transfer's resume file. API35: restart restores a seeding transfer stopped at 100%. Not proven: real process kill on device, low-storage write failures, physical device.

## October 25 local engine web seeds and real Internet download

Building this evidence exposed two defects in the merged engine: libtorrent ignored a bdecoded torrent's BEP 19 `url-list` (zero HTTP requests to a loopback seed), and status readback omitted the torrent name, so the on-phone screen would have shown blank titles. Both are fixed. The public job used the existing collector to fetch licensed Commons `File:Example.ogg`, deleted its own payload copy, and the engine downloaded the file from the official `upload.wikimedia.org` HTTPS URL as its **only** source; libtorrent piece verification and the SHA-256 matched (`CHUNKS_LOCAL_ENGINE_WEBSEED` marker; the test is required not to skip). This is real Internet HTTP(S) transfer on a Linux runner, not a BitTorrent swarm, tracker or Android-device download.

It shows an ongoing low-importance notification with **Stop all**, stops itself when nothing is running, is not sticky (after process death transfers come back stopped and are never auto-restarted), and on the Android 15+ `onTimeout` pauses every transfer and stops instead of being killed. ## October 25 local engine magnet links

[#128](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/128) merged `3fea941bc6ee38d30e6566047c4b61a3d74ae161`; exact source `8d1cb2a9a6d510644db012428fbffb55acceab81` passed [Android/JVM 40246020971](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40246020971) (JVM only; the magnet field has no instrumentation test yet). `addMagnet` accepts exactly one v1 `xt=urn:btih:` identity. First attempt used libtorrent's `stop_when_ready`, which a JVM test showed pauses a magnet **before** any metadata arrives (stuck in `DOWNLOADING_METADATA`). The magnet now runs in **upload mode** (no payload piece requests) until a `METADATA_RECEIVED` alert, then is paused with upload mode cleared and waits for Start. A JVM loopback test shows metadata fetched from a peer with 0 payload bytes downloaded, then a byte-exact download after Start; malformed and multi-identity magnets are refused. Magnets without metadata are never persisted. DHT stays off by default, so only the magnet's trackers and explicit peers are used. ## October 25 first protected Seedhost Kotlin adapter evidence

After the owner added the development URL as the workflow default on `main`, [Kotlin adapter smoke 40246488864](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40246488864) passed at `0a9d6a071e65d424fba2e3ca1bf75db919547f6a`: the actual `RTorrentRemote` negotiated authentication over HTTPS, read the version and the **complete main view** with unique valid identities, non-negative sizes and non-blank names. The [read-only probe with inventory 40246490467](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40246490467) read **436 transfers** and built the QA catalogue (3 representative cases) as a 14-day artifact. Not yet evidence of: paged listing, file listings, writes, latency, or Android-to-Seedhost.

[#130](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/130) merged `692070e7e222367ea734f12a8d2f78a4c1aa2806`. Its read-only live run [40247072785](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40247072785) on the owner's development Seedhost observed: rTorrent **0.9.8**; every method Chunks uses advertised (`d.multicall2`, `download_list`, `system.multicall`, `load.raw_verbose`, `load.verbose`, `d.erase`, `d.start`/`d.stop`, `f.multicall`, `f.priority.set`, `d.update_priorities`, `directory.default`, `d.directory.set`); **`directory.default` empty**; 436 transfers (366 started, 372 complete, ≈4.1 TB, longest name 117 chars) with rich rate fields; `d.multicall2` list 779 ms, paged list 3,741 ms with **exact parity**; two repeated fresh-HTTPS reads ~710 ms each with identical identity sets; 25 sampled file listings (24 multi-file) whose sizes sum to the transfer size. `SeedhostLiveAcceptanceTest` adds read-only fast/paged parity, repeated-read stability, latency lines and sampled file listings, plus an opt-in (`apply_mutation`) round trip on one unique synthetic torrent under `<default>/chunks-acceptance`: stopped Add, duplicate refusal, file priority off/restore, Start/Stop, exact-target session erase, and an assertion that the inventory identity set is restored. Starting the synthetic transfer may leave one tiny file in that test folder; no existing transfer or data is touched.

**First live write attempt ([40247537607](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40247537607), `92a5a2a4`):** read-only checks repeated identically. One unique synthetic `.torrent` (`4c591bc12e73beaf161c5742e0826afc57cf28e6`, `chunks-acceptance-40247537607.txt`) was sent with `load.raw_verbose` and `d.directory.set` to a **not-yet-existing** `chunks-acceptance` subfolder of the inventory's common parent. The server acknowledged it without a fault (`PendingVerification`), but the hash was not listed in `main` within ~10 s, so the run failed closed: no retry, nothing erased. The next run reconciles that hash read-only (main view + direct `d.name`) and targets the existing common folder.

**Add diagnosis ([40248045219](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40248045219), [40248970109](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40248970109)):** the first uncertain hash was reconciled read-only: absent from `main` and unknown to `d.name`, so no orphan exists. A second round trip targeting the **existing** shared folder was also acknowledged but never retained. A variant comparison then sent five unique synthetic Adds: `load.raw_verbose` with no commands, with a quoted `d.directory.set`, and with `d.directory_base.set`; `load.raw`; and a synthetic **magnet** via `load.verbose`. **All returned `0` and none was retained** (exact `d.name` lookup for 15 s each). The host acknowledges load requests and then drops every new transfer, independent of metainfo, directory command or verbosity. This is a host-side behaviour, not a Chunks encoding defect (the same calls work on disposable rTorrent 0.9.8). Read-only event-hook inspection follows. Chunks correctly reports these as unverified, never as success, and never retried.

**Host hooks and ruTorrent upload ([40249669223](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40249669223), [40250768610](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40250768610)):** read-only inspection found `directory.default` = `~/downloads` (tilde, so Chunks correctly declines to treat it as an absolute Place), a host-specific `1_prepare` handler on `event.download.inserted_new` and `inserted_session` beside ruTorrent plugins (ratio, throttle, trackers, autolabel, addtime), and ruTorrent `erasedata0/1` on `event.download.erased`. `erasedata` deletes payload only for transfers ruTorrent marked "delete with data"; Chunks' session erase never sets that mark, but this host-side hook is why session-only Remove must stay explicit about its scope. [#136](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/136) added a stopped Add through ruTorrent's own `php/addtorrent.php`; on this host it was **also dropped** (outcome `PendingVerification`, never listed or found by `d.name`). Six independent Add routes, including the one the ruTorrent web UI uses, are acknowledged and then discarded. That points to an account/host condition (e.g. storage quota or transfer limit enforced on insert), not to Chunks. Live write acceptance (Start/Stop, priority, erase) is **blocked on the owner** confirming that a manual web-UI Add currently works on this Seedhost. No orphan transfers were created; every attempt was reconciled by exact hash.

## October 26 first live write acceptance on the development Seedhost

**Correction:** the earlier "all Add routes are dropped, probably a quota" reading was wrong. The owner confirmed web-UI Adds worked, and [diagnosis 40254131404](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40254131404) found the real cause: **the host silently drops torrents with no tracker (`announce`)**. Torrents with a loopback or an Internet Archive announce URL were retained, listed, stopped, in the exact folder, and cleanly erased. The synthetic fixtures had no tracker.

[#141](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/141) merged `073c36a2c2497da3d9a3e3e93d46e5938579cabe` (build 59fa1e35 passed Android/JVM). Run [40254914585](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40254914585) at that SHA, `apply_mutation=true`, one unique synthetic torrent `92c2ec07d84e0fa41d1f4ea77d387570388f824e` (`chunks-acceptance-40254914585.txt`) in the inventory's common parent folder:
- stopped Add via `load.raw_verbose`: **VerifiedStopped** (exact hash, folder, stopped);
- duplicate Add refused with no second load;
- file priority off then restore: both **Verified**, final priority equal to the original;
- Start **Verified**, Stop **Verified**;
- exact-target session erase: **VerifiedRemoved**;
- inventory identity set restored (437 before and after).

This is the first real-Seedhost write acceptance of Add, duplicate refusal, per-file priority, Start/Stop and session-only Remove through the Kotlin adapter. It is HTTPS from a GitHub runner, not a phone. The ruTorrent upload form was retried with a tracker-less torrent only, so that route is not separately accepted here. Each synthetic transfer holds a tiny file; Start may leave one such file beside real data, no existing transfer was touched.

## October 26 tracker-less Add warning

[#143](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/143) merged `1a04c1c1d651746777601d2ae25ff9d5b5b1bdfe`; exact head `c894759d` passed Android/JVM [40263638982](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40263638982).

Following the Seedhost finding that tracker-less torrents are silently discarded, `TorrentMetainfoPreview.trackerCount` counts distinct http(s)/udp URLs in `announce`/`announce-list` (malformed entries ignored, never fatal), and `magnetTrackerCount` counts distinct `tr=` parameters. The remote Add confirmation card shows an explicit warning when a `.torrent` or magnet has no tracker; the on-phone review notes that peers then depend on DHT or web sources. JVM tests cover counting and deduplication.

## October 26 local engine per-file selection

[#144](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/144) merged `bc7c97e07ee4e070046a1b62ac7c8e292b07e40e`; exact head `11f5c0ba` passed Android/JVM [40265976021](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40265976021) and API35 [40265975993](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40265975993).

`LocalTorrentEngine.files()` lists user files with progress and selection, hiding BEP 47 pad files that hybrid torrents contain (the first test saw 4 entries for 2 real files). `setFileWanted()` sets libtorrent priority IGNORE/DEFAULT and is `VERIFIED` only after readback. JVM loopback test: a skipped 1.3 MB file is not completed while the other file downloads byte-exact; re-selecting it reads back as wanted; a later session completes the kept partial data byte-exact. Finding: in this tracker-less rig, once the leecher reached "finished" it never regained its peer after re-selection within the same session, even with explicit reconnects and a seeder restart. In-session resumption after re-selecting a file is therefore **unproven** (real swarms re-announce through trackers and DHT). The test also showed `fileProgress` can report a file complete before libtorrent's disk thread has written it, so tests wait for the bytes on disk. The on-phone card gets a Files toggle with 48dp checkbox rows ("Untick a file to skip downloading it. Already downloaded parts are kept.").

## October 26 local engine speed limits

[#146](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/146) merged `98bf1455bf653ad96e496a7345894e6c8c33498d`; exact head `9dd3f5f4` passed Android/JVM [40267175139](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40267175139). The speed-limit UI has no emulator or screenshot evidence yet.

`LocalTorrentEngine.setRateLimits()` applies session-wide download/upload limits (bytes/s, 0 unlimited) and returns what libtorrent reads back; `LocalRateLimits` refuses negatives. JVM tests: limits start unlimited, read back exactly and reset. A real loopback transfer with the seeder's upload capped at 128 KiB/s took longer than 60 % of the theoretical ≈10 s; with the same test but libtorrent's default peer classes the transfer finished too fast and the test **failed**, confirming that libtorrent exempts local-network and loopback peers by default. `LocalEngineConfig.rateLimitLocalPeers` (tests only) moves every address into the global class. The on-phone screen gains a "Speed limits" section (KiB/s, blank = unlimited, persisted, applied on engine start) that states local-network devices are not limited. No Internet-swarm or physical-device measurement.

## October 26 library copy of finished local transfers

PR [#147](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/147), head `1a5e7344`, passed Android/JVM [40269473573](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40269473573) and API35 [40269473595](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40269473595) with the new `LocalLibraryCopyInstrumentedTest`.

Two unrelated failures came up on the way and were root-caused in the PR:
- DHT readback: run `40267927242` failed because a 2 s DHT start readback was too short for a loaded runner; the default is now 5 s and the test allows 15 s.
- Atlas alias editor: run `40268777188` failed because the atlas test's positional tap on "Change list name" missed the editor; the test now invokes the button's click action and waits.
- Atlas search: run `40270137679` failed because the IME text input for search sometimes left the queue unfiltered; the test now sets the query with the field's SetText action and waits for it. The installed-app atlas therefore no longer exercises IME typing into search.

`LibraryCopier` copies a finished transfer's wanted files into a **new** folder inside a user-chosen destination, leaving the originals in place so seeding continues (library copy is distinct from the engine's backing data). It reuses the ZIP extractor's protocol and journal format:
- write-ahead journal;
- a random partial folder;
- one non-replacing publish rename (now the shared `ZipExtractor.publish`);
- rollback of journaled entries only;
- `ZipExtractor.reconcile` after process death.

Inputs are refused before any write when:
- a path is unsafe;
- a source is a symlink, outside the transfer folder, missing or not its final size;
- names collide case-insensitively, or a file and a folder share a name.

Each source is SHA-256 hashed while copying and re-hashed afterwards. JVM tests use real files:
- a source changed in already-copied bytes fails and leaves nothing;
- write failure, cancellation and simulated process death roll back via reconcile;
- an existing same-named folder is never merged ("Show S01 (2)").

Android: "Copy to a folder…" appears on finished on-phone transfers, with the same review card in copy wording. Copy journals live in `library-copies/`, so interrupted operations report their kind. A new API35 test copies a real seeding engine transfer into the debug DocumentsProvider and checks that the engine is still seeding afterwards. Not covered: the system picker, vendor/SD/cloud providers and a physical phone.

## October 26 Seedhost ruTorrent upload Add (tracker-bearing)

[#148](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/148) merged `b5d9514d5ee52526e208749ea57906d7d9731a41`. Manual protected run [40271734379](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40271734379) on that SHA with `apply_mutation=true`. A synthetic torrent with a loopback announce, uploaded through ruTorrent's `addtorrent.php` form (`loadStoppedViaRuTorrentUpload`), produced:
- **VerifiedStopped**: listed, `started=false`, directory equal to the chosen base, name matching;
- exact-hash session erase **VerifiedRemoved**;
- inventory unchanged.

The same run repeated the magnet Add and the `.torrent` round trip, all Verified. Both XML-RPC `load.*` and the ruTorrent upload route are therefore accepted on this host for torrents that carry a tracker. Latency in this run: `d.multicall2` list ≈1.1 s and paged listing ≈6.0 s for 437 transfers.


## October 26 modern rTorrent compatibility: accepted 0.16, unresolved Alpine 0.15

[#150](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/150) merged as `ac8103e6e1514362c6763774a6cf6dca50ea322d`. Exact workflow source `2b9d2c3e6291c95fa260bdaaf634527d14ba6186` passed [disposable real-server CI 40368534273](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40368534273) with two successful matrix jobs: legacy Ubuntu rTorrent 0.9.8 and materially newer Arch Linux rTorrent 0.16.x. Each runs the actual Kotlin adapter read-only and stopped Add/duplicate, file-priority, Start/Stop and session-only Remove acceptance against throwaway rTorrent/SCGI, with no production credentials/media. The PR's later documentation-only edit added the explicit negative result to the compatibility matrix. This is **not** a physical-device or managed Seedhost compatibility claim.

Alpine 3.22 rTorrent 0.15.2 was separately attempted but **failed** on [40368041120](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40368041120). The rTorrent PID remained alive, but nginx saw SCGI reset/prematurely closed upstream response headers and all Kotlin RPC operations failed HTTP 502. Track the unsupported build in [#151](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/issues/151); do not call the whole 0.15/0.16 family compatible. The accepted passing matrix excludes Alpine until diagnosed.

## October 26 bounded local foreground status polling (#161)

[#161](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/161) merged as `de2a14e293059108b740d7baf84f3e006eefddb3`. Exact tested PR source `e27b3ea606030c04f51c0f7c889a5d454813197d` passed [Android foundation build/JVM 40402817148](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40402817148). A new atomic admission gate permits at most one scheduled local-engine `list()` observation in flight, dropping redundant 2-second ticks instead of building an unbounded single-thread executor backlog. Gate release is in `finally`, including errors, and an executor rejection during shutdown also releases it. Deterministic JVM checks cover 20,000 rejected overlapping ticks and post-error re-entry. This does **not** establish bounded duration of a *single* stalled native read, Android service exception injection, explicit Stop-all during a permanent native hang, process-kill/Doze/FGS quota or physical device acceptance. Those remain #5.

## October 26 independent local copy and ZIP destination integrity (#163/#164)

- [#163](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/163) merged `6a5820a441cc67cbafe447b8a065ec8a85585b0e`. Exact PR source `c6df239662d19b9b2c9c0eb7910f229717c3d4ce` passed [Android build/JVM 40403915193](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40403915193). Before approving a completed local-torrent library copy, Chunks now queries one file past its 10,000-record cap, refusing copy if entry 10,001 exists. Regression tests cover exactly 10,000 versus 10,001 plus wanted/incomplete/skipped files. This avoids falsely publishing a partial multi-file torrent as a complete library copy. It is **not** paged complete source inventory or real >10k-torrent acceptance.
- [#164](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/pull/164) merged `a857392d5d4d822e4600de9c62d773a846417558`. Exact PR source `3456258ba248278d6e56f58aae87b3308976f65c` passed [Android build/JVM 40404569005](https://github.com/gamesbyian/Chunks/actions/runs/40404569005). After writing each output, the storage adapter now reopens **actual destination bytes** before publication: ZIP extraction verifies stored size plus CRC32; independent library copy verifies stored size plus SHA-256 against the copied source, in addition to rehashing source to notice concurrent changes. Missing, corrupted, truncated or refused readback triggers existing journaled rollback rather than a published success. JVM tests inject a storage provider that acknowledges writes but corrupts later reads.
- **Still unproven:** Android API35 instrumentation of this *new* readback path, ordinary external SD/cloud DocumentsProviders, actual physical handset/process/power-loss recovery, another actor rewriting the document after readback, and end-to-end completed data/seeding state after publishing. JVM/provider-fault tests and compile checks cannot close those gates. Keep #7/#70 open.
